ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u48	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I was asked by Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present the opinion of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice against the draft government law on the principles of recognition of professional qualifications acquired in the Member States of the European Union. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! This project, the government project, is in fact a consequence of the procedures and actions initiated by the Polish Parliament, the Sejm already actually in January this year, when a project was sent to the social partners for consultation. This project, a government project on the recognition of professional qualifications, is really a project aimed at adapting Polish law to European Union law. In fact, the aim of the project is to implement the rules on the recognition of professional qualifications following the entry into force of Directive No 55 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 2013, 20 November 2013, amending the 2005 Directive on the recognition of professional qualifications and Regulation No 1024 of the European Union of 2012 on administrative cooperation through the Internal Market Information System. This project, a project prepared by the current government, as I have pointed out, is a continuation of the work that started definitely earlier. The intention is, in fact, to introduce and define rules for the recognition of professional qualifications for the pursuit of regulated professions and for the taking up or pursuit of regulated activities acquired in countries other than the Republic of Poland within the European Union, but this also applies to the Member States of the European Agreement on Free Trade EFTA and the Swiss Confederation. The draft law also lays down rules for the provision of cross-border services and, above all, lays down rules for applying for a European Professional Card. It is these rules that are really meant to lead to the situation where this movement of staff, the movement of workers within the European Union, will be reduced as little as possible. What actually supports these actions is, firstly, the introduction of procedures for the recognition of professional qualifications. It is an opportunity or the introduction of skill tests, I note, standardized tests, that is also to allow and regulate the rules for carrying out adaptation traineeships. On the other hand, the fundamental issue is the introduction of a European Professional Card, which will actually allow access to the profession. In combination with IMI, i.e. the Internal Market Information System, it will also allow supervision and control of documents or certificates, statements which are necessary to obtain such legitimacy. It is also important that it will be possible, through this IMI system, to introduce what we call the warning procedure. Thus, in the case of persons who lose their ability to pursue a profession or their access to the profession will be restricted, such action will be taken through this system. For the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice [[♪ The bell ♪]] I request further investigation of this bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u49	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform will be presented by Wojciech Ziamniak. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u50	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government draft law on the recognition of professional qualifications acquired in the Member States of the European Union, print 108. My previous speaker has already mentioned the basic objectives of the bill, so I will not quote here the proposed amendments. The overarching objective of the changes is to simplify administrative procedures for the recognition of professional qualifications where possible, including the introduction of new solutions to facilitate recognition of qualifications in those professions where there is no harmonisation of education in the European Union, and to improve the availability of information on procedures and support for citizens. In addition, the rules are intended to facilitate professional mobility for persons who have not yet obtained full professional rights - in the current legal order they are not covered by EU rules on recognition of qualifications. In addition, the proposed law will clarify on the basis of past experience certain specific issues within the framework of the current regulations, which raised interpretation difficulties or caused practical problems. The draft law was examined in committee and all agreed to the amendments made. It can be said that the parliamentary club raises no objections, simply supports the project in its entirety and requests that the High Sejm adopt a law. Thank you.

The main objective of the initiative is to facilitate the mobility of professionals and the provision of services in regulated professions in the Member States of the European Union by simplifying administrative procedures for the recognition of professional qualifications for the pursuit of regulated professions or activities acquired in the Member States of the European Union outside the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as increasing access to information on these procedures using the opportunities offered by modern technologies, including the electronicisation of procedures, introducing new solutions to facilitate the recognition of qualifications in those professions where there is no harmonisation of education in the European Union, facilitating mobility for young people, regulating partial access to the profession, improving the safety of service recipients, especially in the area of health and education of minors. The proposal to modernise the system of mutual recognition of professional qualifications aims to remove existing administrative barriers and to improve and facilitate the situation of migrants. These actions, which took place in the previous term of office, which led to the election of two judges of the Constitutional Tribunal... [[Game in the O.R.]] [[Shh...]] ...but also later the ruling of the tribunal, which as it were in the period of entry into force of the various amendments, shows that the Constitutional Tribunal in this case does not want to close the case, which for the past two months causes us to have a constitutional crisis in Poland. Those changes that have been proposed... [[Shh...]] ...then they're going to settle this situation, explain it, and settle it. The key provisions in this case are that the President of the Constitutional Tribunal should not be able to decide in any way, in such a way as to determine the time limits for the hearing and in such a way as to establish the compositions of the different chambers of judgment, so that, as a result, a decision can be reached which is expected in advance. The purpose of the amendments we make is for the Court to take decisions, to take decisions by qualified majority, so that there is no situation such as the vote of one judge whether the two Judges decide whether the act is constitutional or not. We are also introducing solutions which will result in the Constitutional Tribunal ruling in the order of influence of the cases before the Constitutional Tribunal. So the key is not to be able to juggle cases, so that some cases at the Constitutional Court are or are not settled for two, three, five years, and others are settled within a month or three weeks, as has been the case so far. This situation is intended to settle this matter. It should also be noted that amendments are tabled which aim to eliminate, as I said earlier, the possibility of tampering with the time limits of hearings, the composition of the Constitutional Tribunal and the procedure in individual cases. This amendment is therefore intended to introduce certain clear rules on what cases will be dealt with. We believe that this amendment will put an end to the constitutional crisis that we have in the last month as a result of the Civil Platform. What is also extremely important is that we limit the possibility for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to be employed in various other positions, as has been the case so far. It should not be that the judges of the tribunal, who are well paid, who earn more than 20,000. PLN per month, at the same time they are employed many jobs and ruling at the Constitutional Court is, as it were, an addition to their activities. We want judges to focus on case law, to focus on judgment, because they are designated to do so, to have qualifications and competences, and, moreover, after their work at the Constitutional Court has ceased, they have the right of retirement granted by the legislator and 70% their salary, i.e. they, during these nine years of working in the Court, should focus on the judgment and focus on ensuring that cases are resolved quickly and in a timely manner. We also believe that these rules will make it impossible for us to have such a corpse as before. We have come up against some conclusions from experts who have come to the conclusion that the full composition of the Chamber in any case can slow down the work of the tribunal. Therefore, our amendments, which we have tabled, aim to ensure that the composition of the seven judges of the Constitutional Tribunal resolves legal questions and complaints of citizens. In conclusion, the parliamentary club supports the report submitted. At the same time, I would like to inform you that we are still submitting [[♪ The bell ♪]] clarification amendments. Thank you very much.

Up to now, three formations have been allowed to process proposals to examine the compatibility of the law with the Constitution, because the law provides for a 5-person composition. This, ladies and gentlemen, is gonna be a crazy lengthening process. We already have a long waiting period. I know that the rapporteur said that it is possible to proceed every day that the hearings can be held every day. It's not like that, ladies and gentlemen. We have to prepare for trial. I myself know that the trial is a place where the judge has to be prepared, and in this case we are ruling, please, on the compatibility of the laws with the Constitution. This requires preparation in particular. But for this to be limited, it was added during the meeting of the Legislative Commission a draft provision which talks about waiting for a hearing of three months from the time of delivery of the notices and, in the case of the formation of the judge in full composition, six months. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] Ladies and gentlemen, this is an awful, unnecessary extension of the procedure. Nowadays, notification to the parties to the proceedings takes place quickly. Procedures are already in place which provide for electronic delivery. It's really fast. So it was extended from 14 days to 3 months and six months. During this time, the absolute majority of this time will be unobtrusive, because the notification will be delivered. This will prolong the unnecessary course of action. Therefore, I hope - in view of the fact that amendments have been tabled - that this will be changed. Please also note that we have postponed the decision to terminate the mandate or to resign from office to the Sejm. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Constitutional Act only provides that the Sejm elects the judges of the Tribunal. I know I'm out of time, Marshal, but I'm gonna finish.

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u181	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present my position on the establishment of the Year of Sienkiewicz on behalf of the Modern. Henryk Adam Aleksander Pius Sienkiewicz, nicknamed "Litwos" was born on May 5, 1846 in Wola Okrzejska in the district of Łukow in a poor family of nobles who sealed the coat of arms of Oskar after a sword originating from the Tatars settled in Lithuania. Polish novelist, novelist and publicist, Nobel Prize laureate in literature from 1905 for his entire work, one of the most popular Polish writers of the turn of the 19th and 20th century. He earned his profession through medical, legal and finally philosophical-historical studies. He had a thorough knowledge of literature and Old Polish language, which worked so well in his novelism. In 1869 he debuted as a journalist. He made a living as a reporter and columnist. He became a co-owner for a loan of money to a two-week-old Niwa. In February 1876 he and Helena Modrzejewska and a group of friends traveled to the United States, where he and his friends attempted to create an agricultural commune in California. From this period came letters from traveling to America printed in the Polish Gazeta, which gained wide recognition of readers. 8 September 1877 Sienkiewicz published an article in the daily Evening Post, entitled "Poland and Russia," in which he attacked the Russian authorities' policies. Under the influence of traveling to the United States, he wrote some more songs: by steppe, by bread, by the land of gold, by the Lantern, by the Memory of Maripose, by Sachem. The correspondence from the United States published in the Polish press gained wide recognition and aroused interest in him. The writer's success was well painted by Bolesław Prussia in an article about what Mr Sienkiewicz does with a more beautiful half of Warsaw. He wrote: "As soon as she returned from America, almost each of the ladies, passing by the street, accused almost every taller and handsome man of being Sienkiewicz. I'm sorry, Finally, meeting hairstyles a la Sienkiewicz, knowing that young gentlemen one by one grow Spanish women, trying to have sketchy features and breakfast skin, I decided to meet him himself. Between 1882 and 1887 Sienkiewicz became editor-in-chief, editor of the journal "Słowo" in which on 12 May 1883 the first episode of the new historical novel was published, a series called "Trilogy," including the turbulent history of Poland from the 17th century during the wars with the Tatars, the Swedish and the Turks. Based on Walter Scott's romantic historical novel, [[♪ The bell ♪]] The works of Alexander Dumas and old Polish literature, Sienkiewicz created a model combining an adventurous plot with a wide picture of customs and historical events to refresh the hearts of compatriots. In 1894, the first chapters of Quo vadis were created. The book edition appeared soon after and made a dizzy career across Europe. The book is still extremely popular today and has been translated into many languages, including Arabic or Japanese, as well as Esperanto. Quo vadis, once again, was adapted and staged on the boards of theatres, and was filmed for the first time in 1913. The implementation of Jerzy Kawalerowicz from 2001 remains the most expensive production of Polish cinematography.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u182	Finish your work, sir.

He was the president of the money of caution for writers and journalists, for whom he defended the interests of writers, helped to obtain loans, financial assistance. He represented writers, organized creative meetings, discussion evenings or cultural events. He was also a co-founder of the money they still have. Mianowski for people working in the scientific field, thanks to which he provided support and assistance to scientific publishing companies, aiming to increase the level of education in the country. In 1889 he funded a scholarship to them. Maria Sienkiewiczowa for writers living in difficult material conditions. He also founded a church in Zakopane and a children's tuberculosis sanatorium. He spoke on important public and political issues. He repeatedly opposed Prussian Germanization policy and demanded Autonomy for the Kingdom of Poland. His last important initiative was to organize a relief committee for the victims of the war in Poland, which was founded in Switzerland after the outbreak of World War I. The co-organizers were Andrzej Osuchowski and Ignacy Jan Paderewski. The Committee has collected food, medicines, clothes and money in large quantities. He had many successes not only as a writer but, above all, as a man, a man full of morality and empathy. His activity was an expression of patriotism and love of his homeland. I am convinced that the entire High Chamber, unanimously, maybe by acclamation, will support this resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Feliks Nowowiejski is a form of musical culture, which by means of notes fought for memory and promotion of Polish culture. Although he was not born in Poland because he was born in Eastern Prussia, in Barczew, his attitude and activity in favour of the country showed that he was a true patriot. He played in the orchestra of the Grenadier regiment, where he extolled and supported marches, parades and battalion parades with his songs. In 1898, he received the first prize of The British Musician at a composer's competition in London for his song "Under the banner of peace," which he was to call for peace in his homeland according to the title. In 1902, he received a prize for the oratorio and the return of the prodigal son and the romantic overture. Through his music he paid tribute to Polish culture and called for a fight for his homeland. His songs aroused emotions, warmed up the fight and commemorated the most important historical events. On 15 July 1910 in Krakow, on the 500th anniversary of the Battle of Grunwald, gathered on pl. In front of the exposed monument of Władysław Jagiełła, the choirs under the baton of Felix Nowowiejski sang for the first time the words of "Roty" by Maria Konopnicka, to which the poem Nowowiejski wrote music. Rota was created in 1908 under the outrage of Polish persecutions in the Prussian partition from which it originated. It was only two years later that Nowowiejski arranged a melody for her. Rota was also sung during martial law. After the war, Felix Nowowiejski settled in Poznań and was actively involved in the revival of the cultural life of the city - organized symphonic and choral concerts, performed with organ recitals. With his songs, he encouraged the hearts of Poles, encouraged them to fight against the enemy and brought cultural elements into their lives. He was a valued figure not only in Poland, but also outside the borders of our country. His works appreciated the most outstanding figures of musical culture, and to this day it is an inspiration for creators. An expression of gratitude and homage for his work and work is his name being named after the symphony orchestra in Olsztyn, the Music Academy in Bydgoszcz and music schools. in Gdansk, Gniezno, Szczecin and Tuchola. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u199	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Modern Club on the establishment of 2016 with Felix Nowowiejski's Year. In 2016, there were 70. The anniversary of the death of Felix Nowowiejski. We are convinced that this anniversary is an excellent opportunity to pay tribute to our distinguished countryman. Felix Nowowiejski was born on 7 February 1877 in Wartembork, now Barczewo, in Warmia. He died on 18 January 1946 in Poznań. Polish composer, conductor, educator, organist, virtuoso, organizer of musical life, papal chamberlain. He gained basic musical education at a high school in St. Lipka, then in Olsztyn, where he started working in the Prussian orchestra of the Grenadiers' regiment. He composed works for military orchestras and amateur bands. He continued his studies in the organ and composition classes in Berlin and in the church music school in Ratyzbona. He attended lectures in musicology and aesthetics. Since 1906, he has been in Berlin, where he has been associated with Polish forces. He conducted lively compositional and virtuoso activities. He traveled to Warsaw, Cracow, Lviv and the Middle East several times, where he performed, among others. in the presence of the patriarch of Jerusalem. In 1907 a premiere performance took place in Usti on the Elbe of the oratorio of Quo vadis. This work was enthusiastically accepted throughout Europe and America. By 1939 it was done more than 200 times. On Polish lands for the first time in 1912 in Warsaw. In 1910 he joined the celebration of the 500th anniversary of the victory at Grunwald and the unveiling in Krakow of a monument founded by Ignacy Jan Paderewski, a composer of the text by Maria Konopnicka. The original title of the song "Password." After the war, he settled in Poznań and became a part of the reborn cultural life of the city. He taught at the conservatory in Poznań at the church music department. He conducted the orchestra, conducted the organ class and taught improvisation. In 1924, the opera in Poznań premiered the Baltic Legend, which he performed 50 times in one season. In 1920 and 1921 he organized a 400-person national choir. He's been in jury teams for a lot of competitions. Traditionally, on Thursday, his organ recitals were broadcast by the Poznań radio to the whole of Poland. In 1929, at the reunion of the singer, Nowowiejski conducted the choir composed of 20 thousand. The singers who performed his Psalm 136. Jerusem the Homeland. In 1934, he conducted his opera - the ballet of folk paintings in Paris as part of a series of composers' concerts. He led the City Symphony Orchestra in Poznań, promoting contemporary music. In September 1939, fearing [[♪ The bell ♪]] Before his arrest he hid in the Hospital of Sisters of Elżbetanek and then in Krakow, where he returned to Poznań in 1945. Soon he died and was buried on the Poznań Rock in the Krypt of the Decent Church of St. Wojciech. Feliks Nowowiejski was the winner of many prestigious awards received, among others. London, Bonn, Chicago and Lviv. He also received the dignity of the papal chamberlain, in 1936 the Commander's Cross of the Order of Polonia Restitut and the Honorary Badge of the Unification of Polish Singing and Musical Teams. Some of his manuscripts were burned in 1945 during the war, many of them were lost. The Modern Club will support the resolution on the establishment of the year 2016 by Felix Nowowiejski. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u225	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am very pleased to submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of 2016 by the Year of the 1050th anniversary of the Polish Baptism. This project was submitted by the Law and Justice Club. I have been authorized to represent applicants in the work on this project. The Marshal of the Sejm forwarded this project to the Committee on Culture and Media. The Commission carried out its first reading and examined the project on 10 December 2015. The Commission adopted the draft resolution unanimously. Here's the content of the project. In the 1050th anniversary of the Prince's baptism, the First Sejm of the Republic of Poland honors the memory of this crucial event in the history of our Nation. The baptism of the ruler of Polan and his court, and consequently the baptism of Poland, took place on the Passover Eve, April 14, 966. This act was decisive for the process of uniting Polish tribes under the power of the Piasts and forming the Christian identity of our Nation. St. John Paul II spoke to Polish bishops on 14 February 1998 about the importance of baptism: 'Poland entered the circle of Christian culture of the West and began to build its future on the foundation of the Gospel. Since then, we have become full members of the European family of nations with all the consequences. Together with other peoples of Europe we are co-creators and heirs of its rich history and culture'. The celebrations of Sacrum Millennium Poloniae celebrated 50 years ago in 1966 were not only a mass demonstration of faith, but also an expression of resistance to communist power. By this they became a milestone on the Polish road to freedom. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland establishes 2016 as the Year of the Jubilee of the 1050th anniversary of the Polish Baptism. It also hopes that anniversary celebrations will be a way of reconciliation and reconstruction of the community based on the more than a thousand years of Christian tradition of our Nation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt a draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The silent activities enabled communication with the country by air and the carrying out of sabotage-diversity actions against the invaders. Until the end of 1944 316 Silencers were cast into Poland, of which 112 gave their lives for their free homeland. Several more Polish soldiers - paratroopers - were sent to other occupied countries in Europe. Silent, people who received special professional and specialized training were the elite of the Polish Armed Forces. Due to the fact that their merits in the fights for independence of Poland were extraordinary, and the performances of war exceptional, should be permanently in the pantheon of our national heroes. Knowledge about the great achievements and sacrifice of the Silent must be disseminated, especially among young Poles. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u255	The silent ones were soldiers, partisans of the Polish Armed Forces, landing to occupied Poland during World War II in order to conduct an irregular fight against the German invader and organize and train the Polish resistance movement in the country. Today it is difficult to determine when and under what circumstances the correct name of the AK jumpers was born. During the war, they were the ones that were needed by the growing underground army in the country - soldiers of various weapons, experts, in one word: experts to fight the enemy. They were officers led by air at the disposal of the Home Army. People specially chosen, intelligent, strong, patriotic. In August 1940 the Polish Special Branch was established as part of Unit VI of the Chief Chief Staff. Of all nationalities he was the most independent, had his own connection with the country, his own code and the ability to recruit candidates for this extraordinary responsible service, requiring excellent physical condition and enormous intelligence. Parachutes jumpers for special tasks, i.e. the quiet ones, had very difficult and complicated trainings, whose range included among others.: diversion, guerrilla, intelligence, communications, operations, armored and anti-tank tasks, air services, counterfeiting of documents, etc. For our freedom, our good, and our salvation, they have dedicated themselves to hard service. They were a comprehensively trained staff, which was to be an instructor's elite and to pass on their knowledge and experience to a wide range of soldiers, they were a model for other uniforms. They fought for free Poland using physical and cultural attributes. They did not allow the memory of Poland to fade away. They were everywhere. They participated in almost all major actions. The Gestapo and Abwehr were intimidatingly tracking them. The Warsaw Uprising was attended by 91 silent ones, showing courage and sacrificing their lives to save Poland. They worked at headquarters, on the line, at different command posts, at the communications service, in the production of means of combat. Their sign is an eagle falling into battle, holding a wreath with the sign of Poland Fighting and the inscription: You Homeland, which today is associated with patriotism and fighting for your homeland. Silent people are Poles, who we owe our lives to in free Poland. People strong, educated and full of faith that what they have won for us is the highest value, because as you know, freedom is priceless.

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u259	Thank you. Your Marshal, I apologize for the confusion over me. High Izbo! The question raised concerns trains that provide access to schools and jobs. This type of transport needs are met by transport of a regional nature, i.e. passenger trains. Organizing this type of transport is the task of local governments of voivodships. This follows from the provisions of Article. Paragraph 7 Point 1 (5) of the Law of December 2010 on public collective transport. These regulations clearly indicate that voivodships are the organizers of public collective transport in voivodship and inter-voivodship transports. Therefore, decisions on the shape of rail connections in the regions of the local government unit shall be taken on their own, based on the needs of the local community's transport and taking into account the financial resources available to them. Therefore, the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction does not have an impact on the shape of local transport, since according to the provisions indicated it is not involved in organising and subsidising them. The resort has no legal basis for exerting any pressure on the decisions of the local authorities and on the way in which the entity manages its budget. On the other hand, the responsibility of the Ministry of Transport is the network of passenger transport subsidised from the State budget by the Minister responsible for Transport. However, it is a skeleton of long-distance connections, on the basis of which other transport organisers, including local authorities, shape the local transport offer. This division of tasks results from the provisions of the Act of 16 December 2010 on public collective transport. And now, on the order of the minister responsible for transport in the Częstochowa - Koniecpol - Kraków section in force since 13 December, is operated by long-distance transports of the TLK or Intercity category, but these are trains with a definition of a small number of stops due to the need to ensure as short travel time as possible at long distances. A train of this particular category, if it were standing everywhere, would be a passenger train. I would also like to explain that due to the short deadline for preparing the answers, it was not possible to obtain detailed information concerning the route Częstochowa - Kielce. We have tried here to obtain explanations of the Marshal's offices of Silesian and Świętokrzyskie voivodships, which negotiate with Regional Transports, I stress: not with PKP Regional Transports, as in the question was included, but with Regional Transports, which are not owned by the Minister of Infrastructure. The scope of the transport offer of these Regional Transports is financed by the local government. However, I can promise that we will try to draw the attention of both competent marshal offices to this case effectively. Thank you very much.

On the other hand, as regards the second question concerning the investment of S14, the S14 expressway, the task will be carried out by two subsections: the section from the Łódź-Lublinek junction to the Łódź-Teophile junction and the section from the Łódź-Teophile junction, without this junction, to DK91 in the village of Słowik. The total length of both sections is 28.5 km. The investment will be carried out in the traditional system, i.e. in a system in which the contracting authority is responsible for the design and preparation and the contractor carries out the works. This is quite important because it allows us, the general management, but also the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction to better control this, to better supervise the construction process. On 2 October 2015, a tender procedure was opened for the selection of economic operators of both sections under a restricted tendering procedure. The estimated cost of building the S14 section is PLN 1.5 billion. The funding comes from the state budget and the National Road Fund. And what is also, I think, most important is the fact that the planned deadline for concluding contracts is December 2016. Therefore, the implementation will probably start in 2017 and the completion of works at the turn of 2019 and 2020. As for the midrange tunnel - this was also asked - in order to go straight to the most important information, I will say that as part of the preparatory work for the new perspective, a feasibility study is being developed for the construction of the railway line in the tunnel to the Łódź Fabryczna station, line 15. It is a project included within the framework of the OP IiŚ 2007-2013. The total value of the task is PLN 3 million. Work is currently underway to develop a full feasibility study. So here we are at the beginning of the work. The evaluation of the analyses carried out in the framework of the preparatory project and the selection of the tunnel variant will be carried out in accordance with existing standards, based on the applicable rules and guidelines of the competent authorities. PKP PLK makes every effort to meet the social demands of local authorities, but until the end of the work it is not possible to indicate the specific route of the tunnel and the location of the stops. Once the preparatory work has been completed, it will certainly be possible to comment on this in accordance with the procedures to be assessed. And I hope this public consultation doesn't slow down the process. We have numerous cases in the country that, as it is understandable, some of these or other localizational indications do not like the inhabitants, then there are protests that slow down the investment process. Hopefully, in this case, we can avoid it, but not everything. [[♪ The bell ♪]] depends on the will of the government or the Polish Railway Lines, which carry out this investment. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u317	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In the Lesser Poland Voivodeship, the program of small retention was adopted in 2004 and, unfortunately, it has not yet been implemented. Similar works also continued in other voivodships of southern Poland. A small retention, i.e. the construction of small water reservoirs, is aimed at water retention and protection against flooding, i.e. prolonged water circulation by increasing the capacity to retain rainwater, which has a beneficial impact on the water balance. However, the construction of small tanks, in addition to improving the water balance, is also of social and economic importance as it increases the attractiveness of the area, facilitates the creation of jobs in the tourism sector and thus creates opportunities for an effective fight against unemployment. Well, in the past eight years, unfortunately, any question about the implementation of this programme, for which local governments and voivodships have adopted, because they have seen in this programme the opportunity for sustainable development based on local resources, any questions about this matter have been disposed of by the representatives of the then government. As a reason for failing to implement the programme, it was stated that it was contrary to the requirements of protecting ichiofauna, that it aroused protests by fishermen's operators. Hence my question to the Minister: Minister, can not the small retention programme be implemented? Is it true that it cannot be reconciled with water protection requirements? What are the plans of the current government and the Minister in this regard? Will the government return to the implementation of the small retention programme where local and regional authorities expect this? When and under what conditions could this return occur? Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u318	Thank you very much, Member. Mr Mariusz Gajda, Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, answers the question. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-22-sejm-06-2.u319	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for these questions. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, a moment of reflection. Why have floods and droughts grown so much in recent times? Not only because the climate changes - because it changes - but because the urbanization of the country, among other things, is progressing. construction of roads, construction of houses, parking lots at supermarkets, and there is at this point no countering this in the form of retention tanks. Through the construction of all these paved surfaces, natural retention decreases and this causes a rapid flow of water, especially in submountain and mountain areas, where there is enough really little rain to create a flood wave. So far, the rivers of peace have turned into streams of raging. The only way to really counteract this phenomenon is to stop the water locally. Of course, large tanks are needed on large rivers, but above all, this retention must be created locally and only then will we be able to protect ourselves from flooding and drought. Just a little bit more history. Right after the war, there were about 10,000 people in Poland. small retention tanks. In theory, this is highly appreciated in the Water Law, but in other legal acts it is as if it is prohibited. They're practically construction bans. Almost 40.5 million m3 of water was re-re-re-rated. Finally, I would like to stress that we are really very important to ensure that the water that falls in a given local place is stopped there or that excess water is then used to compensate during drought periods and that these flood losses are as small as possible. The euro is accounted for on the basis of lump sums, i.e. there is no obligation in practice to deliver invoices, there is no obligation to show accounting documents. The beneficiary of the project shall only be accounted for by the results of the actions. And that is the result - on the one hand, the simplification of the method of accounting, on the other hand the focus on results, i.e. the efficient provision of skills, qualifications, on the other hand, it is also a question of the law on the integrated qualification system and the register of qualifications, i.e. putting on results - that is also the element that should contribute on the one hand to increasing flexibility, on the other hand to the resultant approach. So that, speaking of flexibility, we actually have two paradigms, because one is an absorption paradigm, while this paradigm is not just the only paradigm, it should also be accompanied by the implementation of an efficiency paradigm related to the resultant approach, so that EU funds are more correlated with project effects, rather than just focusing on formal and procedural correctness. Of course, we need to increase the mystery about risk-sharing instruments in Poland, it is not complete at the moment, and in the interest of the Polish state and increasing access to innovative drug technologies is to keep risk-sharing instruments secret. Of course, we are preparing at the moment, we have started preparing a document that will be a medical policy of the state. I believe that the main objective of this document should be to reduce the deficit in drug trade with other countries. Poland has very good traditions when it comes to pharmaceutical production. It is a pity that we have this deficit balance, negative, and we will certainly fight it with different kinds of mechanisms. We will block illegal export of medicines refunded from Poland. The changes made have slowed down or limited legal exports to some extent, while illegal exports are flourishing. The only way to stop illegal exports of refunded drugs is to approximate - more or less - the prices of official medicines refunded in Poland to those that are in the West, but through risk instruments we will reduce effective prices, i.e. the costs that the NFZ incurs for the refund of medicines, while maintaining the level of co-payment, at least at an initial level, at the same level. We want to relieve patients and reduce patient subsidies to refunded drugs. This is mainly about the law on medicines for seniors. I answer the Member's question: they will mostly go there - but not all - medicines for which there is an important co-payment on the part of the elderly at the moment, i.e. mainly medicines that are listed today: 30% and 50% co-payments, although not only - will, will also appear, which are on a lump sum today, and from very different groups. The basic principle, of course, is that they are to be medicines that are actually dedicated to diseases of old age, but they will also be medicines that are characterized by low moral gambling and low risk of abuse on the part of healthcare providers.
